````math
\mathbb{E}\big[(v^{\top}\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{t}^{(\mathrm{sig})})^{2}\big]=\tau_{w}^{2}\,\mathbb{E}\!\big[\|\Diag(\bar{\mathbf{n}}_{t})v\|_{2}^{2}\big]=\tau_{w}^{2}\sum_{i=1}^{K}\mathbb{E}[\bar{n}_{t,i}^{2}]\,v_{i}^{2}.
``` |  |

Let $`N_{t,i}:=\sum_{s=1}^{t}\mathbf{1}\{A_{s}=i\}`$. Since
$`N_{t,i}^{2}\geq N_{t,i}`$,
$`\mathbb{E}[\bar{n}_{t,i}^{2}]=\mathbb{E}[N_{t,i}^{2}]/t^{2}\geq\mathbb{E}[N_{t,i}]/t^{2}=\mathbb{E}[\bar{n}_{t,i}]/t`$.
Mixture exploration yields $`\mathbb{E}[\bar{n}_{t,i}]\geq\gamma/K`$, so

|     |                                             |     |       |
|-----|---------------------------------------------|-----|-------|
|     |
       ``` math
       c\ \geq\ \tau_{w}^{2}\,\frac{\gamma}{K\,t}.
       ```                                          |     | (A.5) |

Adding the PSD noise covariance only increases $`\bm{\Sigma}_{gg}`$,
thus
equation [A.5](#A1.E5 "Equation A.5 ‣ Proof. ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
holds.

We have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\bm{\Sigma}_{ng}=\mathbb{E}[\bar{\mathbf{n}}_{t}\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{t}^{\top}]=\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\!\big[\bar{\mathbf{n}}_{t}(\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{t}^{(\mathrm{sig})})^{\top}\big]}_{=\,0}\;+\;\mathbb{E}\!\big[\bar{\mathbf{n}}_{t}(\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{t}^{(\mathrm{noise})})^{\top}\big].
``` |  |

The first term vanishes because $`\mathbf{w}`$ is independent of
$`(\bar{\mathbf{n}}_{t},\{\epsilon_{s}\})`$ and
$`\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{w}]=0`$. For the second term,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\!\big[\bar{\mathbf{n}}_{t}(\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{t}^{(\mathrm{noise})})^{\top}\big]=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{u=1}^{t}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{u}\,\epsilon_{s}\,\mathbf{x}_{s}^{\top}]=\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\sum_{u=s+1}^{t}\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{u}\,\epsilon_{s}\,\mathbf{x}_{s}^{\top}],
``` |  |

where we used $`\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{s}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}]=0`$ to
eliminate $`u\leq s`$. Fix $`s<u`$. Consider “world 0” where
$`\epsilon_{s}`$ is set to $`0`$, and “world 1” the true world. Then

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{u}\,\epsilon_{s}\,\mathbf{x}_{s}^{\top}]=\mathbb{E}\!\big[(\mathbf{p}_{u}^{(1)}-\mathbf{p}_{u}^{(0)})\,\epsilon_{s}\,\mathbf{x}_{s}^{\top}\big],
``` |  |

since
$`\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{p}_{u}^{(0)}\,\epsilon_{s}\,\mathbf{x}_{s}^{\top}]=0`$.
With $`\eta_{u}=c_{0}/u`$, the projected logits satisfy (one-step normal
form)

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbf{y}_{u}=\frac{c_{0}}{u-1}\,\big(\mathbf{V}\,\mathbf{n}_{u-1}+\mathbf{U}\,\mathbf{g}_{u-1}\big)\ \ \Rightarrow\ \ \Delta\mathbf{y}_{u}=\frac{c_{0}}{u-1}\,\mathbf{U}\,(\epsilon_{s}\,\mathbf{x}_{s})
``` |  |

when only $`\epsilon_{s}`$ is perturbed. By
Lemma [A.2](#A1.Thmtheorem2 "Lemma A.2 (Softmax is 1/2-Lipschitz on 𝟏^⟂). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
and the $`(1-\gamma)`$ factor from
equation [3.3](#S3.E3 "Equation 3.3 ‣ Policy Optimization Framework. ‣ 3 Preliminaries ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\mathbf{p}_{u}^{(1)}-\mathbf{p}_{u}^{(0)}\|_{2}\ \leq\ (1-\gamma)\cdot\tfrac{1}{2}\,\|\Delta\mathbf{y}_{u}\|_{2}\ \leq\ (1-\gamma)\cdot\tfrac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{c_{0}}{u-1}\,\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,|\epsilon_{s}|.
``` |  |

Hence

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\big\|\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{x}_{u}\,\epsilon_{s}\,\mathbf{x}_{s}^{\top}]\big\|_{\mathrm{op}}\ \leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\!\big[\|\mathbf{p}_{u}^{(1)}-\mathbf{p}_{u}^{(0)}\|_{2}\,|\epsilon_{s}|\big]`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\ \leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{(1-\gamma)}{2}\,\frac{c_{0}}{u-1}\,\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{s}^{2}]`$ |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\ \leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{(1-\gamma)}{2}\,\frac{c_{0}}{u-1}\,\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}.`$ |  |

Summing $`u=s+1,\dots,t`$ and $`s=1,\dots,t-1`$ gives

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle b\ =\ \big\|\bm{\Sigma}_{ng}\big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}\big\|_{\mathrm{op}}\ \leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{1}{t^{2}}\sum_{s=1}^{t-1}\sum_{u=s+1}^{t}\frac{(1-\gamma)}{2}\,\frac{c_{0}}{u-1}\,\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\ \leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{(1-\gamma)}{2}\,c_{0}\,\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}\cdot\frac{t-1}{t^{2}}`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\ \leq\ `$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{C_{b}}{t},`$ |  | (A.6) |

with
$`C_{b}:=\tfrac{(1-\gamma)}{2}\,c_{0}\,\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,\sigma_{\epsilon}^{2}`$.

For any unit $`(u,v)\in S`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\begin{pmatrix}u\\
v\end{pmatrix}^{\!\top}\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\Big|_{S}\begin{pmatrix}u\\
v\end{pmatrix}\ \geq\ a\|u\|^{2}-2b\|u\|\,\|v\|+c\|v\|^{2}\ \geq\ \lambda_{\min}\!\begin{pmatrix}a&-b\\
-b&c\end{pmatrix}.
``` |  |

Thus

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lambda_{\min}\!\big(\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big|_{S}\big)\ \geq\ \frac{a+c-\sqrt{(a-c)^{2}+4b^{2}}}{2}.
``` |  | (A.7) |

By
equation [A.4](#A1.E4 "Equation A.4 ‣ Proof. ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
equation [A.5](#A1.E5 "Equation A.5 ‣ Proof. ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
and
equation [A.6](#A1.Ex61 "In Proof. ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\sqrt{ac}\ =\ \tau_{w}\,\frac{\gamma}{K\,t},\qquad b\ \leq\ \frac{C_{b}}{t}.
``` |  |

The small cross-block condition
equation [A.3](#A1.E3 "Equation A.3 ‣ Lemma A.7 (Population second moment is positive definite on 𝑆). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
implies $`b<\sqrt{ac}`$ (for all $`t\geq 2`$), hence the $`2\times 2`$
matrix $`\begin{pmatrix}a&-b\\
-b&c\end{pmatrix}`$ is positive definite and the right-hand side of
equation [A.7](#A1.E7 "Equation A.7 ‣ Proof. ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
is strictly positive. Therefore $`\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big|_{S}\succ 0`$. ∎

###### Lemma A.8 (Sample second-moment concentration for $`\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}`$).

Under
Assumption [3.1](#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Assumption 3.1 (Data Coverage and Signal Dominance). ‣ Supervised Pretraining Data Generation. ‣ 3 Preliminaries ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
and $`\mathbf{w}\sim\mathcal{N}({\bm{0}},\tau_{w}^{2}\mathbf{I}_{K})`$,
the normalized statistic
$`\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}:=(\bar{\mathbf{n}}_{t}^{\top},\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{t}^{\top})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{2K}`$
is sub-Gaussian with a dimension-free $`\psi_{2}`$–norm
$`L:=\|\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\|_{\psi_{2}}`$ depending only on
$`(\tau_{w},\sigma_{\epsilon},\gamma)`$ (and not on $`K`$). Let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widehat{\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}}\ :=\ \frac{1}{M}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}^{(m)}\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}^{(m)\top},\qquad\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\ :=\ \mathbb{E}[\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}^{\top}],
``` |  |

where $`\{\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}^{(m)}\}_{m=1}^{M}`$ are i.i.d. copies of
$`\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}`$ generated by independent rollouts of the same
population process (fixed $`\gamma`$). Let $`\underline{\sigma}>0`$ be
the population lower bound from
Lemma [A.7](#A1.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma A.7 (Population second moment is positive definite on 𝑆). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
i.e.,
$`\lambda_{\min}\!\big(\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big|_{S}\big)\geq\underline{\sigma}`$
on $`S:={\bm{1}}^{\perp}\oplus{\bm{1}}^{\perp}`$. Then there is a
universal constant $`C>0`$ such that, for any $`\delta\in(0,1)`$, with
probability at least $`1-\delta`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\big\|\widehat{\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}}-\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big\|_{\mathrm{op}}\ \leq\ C\,L^{2}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{2K+\log(1/\delta)}{M}}\;+\;\frac{2K+\log(1/\delta)}{M}\right).
``` |  | (A.8) |

In particular, if

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
M\ \geq\ C_{\mathrm{mc}}\;\frac{2K+\log(1/\delta)}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}\qquad\text{with}\qquad C_{\mathrm{mc}}:=16\,C^{2}\,L^{4},
``` |  |

then
$`\big\|\widehat{\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}}-\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\,\underline{\sigma}`$,
and consequently

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lambda_{\min}\!\Big(\widehat{\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}}\Big|_{S}\Big)\ \geq\ \tfrac{1}{2}\,\underline{\sigma}.
``` |  | (A.9) |

###### Proof .

The sub-Gaussianity of $`\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}`$ follows from: (i)
$`\bar{\mathbf{n}}_{t}=\tfrac{1}{t}\sum_{s=1}^{t}\mathbf{x}_{s}`$ is an
average of one-hot vectors from a $`\gamma`$-mixture policy, hence
coordinate-wise sub-Gaussian with $`\psi_{2}`$–norm bounded by a
constant depending only on $`\gamma`$; (ii)
$`\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{t}=\tfrac{1}{t}\sum_{s=1}^{t}r_{s}\mathbf{x}_{s}`$
with $`r_{s}=\mathbf{w}^{\top}\mathbf{x}_{s}+\epsilon_{s}`$, where
$`\mathbf{w}`$ is Gaussian independent of $`\{\mathbf{x}_{s}\}`$ and
$`\{\epsilon_{s}\}`$, and $`\{\epsilon_{s}\}`$ is conditionally
sub-Gaussian. Thus $`\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{t}`$ is a sum of sub-Gaussian
vectors with $`\psi_{2}`$–norm controlled by
$`(\tau_{w},\sigma_{\epsilon},\gamma)`$; concatenation preserves
sub-Gaussianity with $`L=\|\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\|_{\psi_{2}}=O(1)`$ in
$`(\tau_{w},\sigma_{\epsilon},\gamma)`$.

For i.i.d. sub-Gaussian samples in $`\mathbb{R}^{d}`$ with $`d=2K`$, the
standard sample covariance operator-norm deviation bound (e.g., matrix
Bernstein / sub-Gaussian covariance concentration)
yields equation [A.8](#A1.E8 "Equation A.8 ‣ Lemma A.8 (Sample second-moment concentration for 𝐳̄_𝑡). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"):

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\big\|\widehat{\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}}-\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big\|_{\mathrm{op}}\ \leq\ C\,L^{2}\!\left(\sqrt{\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{M}}\;+\;\frac{d+\log(1/\delta)}{M}\right).
``` |  |

If $`M\geq C_{\mathrm{mc}}(2K+\log(1/\delta))/\underline{\sigma}^{2}`$,
then
$`\|\widehat{\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}}-\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\underline{\sigma}/2`$.
By Weyl’s inequality on the restriction to $`S`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lambda_{\min}\!\Big(\widehat{\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}}\Big|_{S}\Big)\ \geq\ \lambda_{\min}\!\Big(\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\Big|_{S}\Big)\;-\;\big\|\widehat{\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}}-\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big\|_{\mathrm{op}}\ \geq\ \underline{\sigma}-\tfrac{1}{2}\,\underline{\sigma}\ =\ \tfrac{1}{2}\,\underline{\sigma},
``` |  |

which
is equation [A.9](#A1.E9 "Equation A.9 ‣ Lemma A.8 (Sample second-moment concentration for 𝐳̄_𝑡). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement").
∎

###### Lemma A.9 (Fisher two-channel quadratic: gradient and Hessian).

Consider the *population* Fisher-weighted quadratic from
Eq. equation [4.1](#S4.E1 "Equation 4.1 ‣ Training Objective. ‣ 4 Theoretical Framework for ICPO ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
written in the two-channel parameterization

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})\ :=\ \frac{1}{2}\,\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\!\big(\bm{\Gamma}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\top}\big)\;-\;\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\!\big(\bm{\Gamma}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{y\bar{z}}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\top}\big)\;+\;\mathrm{const},
``` |  | (A.10) |

where $`\bar{\mathbf{W}}\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times 2K}`$,
$`\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}=\mathbb{E}[\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}^{\top}]`$,
$`\bm{\Sigma}_{y\bar{z}}=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{y}_{t+1}\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}^{\top}]`$,
and
$`\bm{\Gamma}=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{F}(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1})]`$.
Then

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})\ `$ | $`\displaystyle=\ \bm{\Gamma}\big(\bar{\mathbf{W}}\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}_{y\bar{z}}\big)\ =:\ \mathbf{E}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times 2K},`$ |  | (A.11) |
|  | $`\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})[\Delta]\ `$ | $`\displaystyle=\ \bm{\Gamma}\,\Delta\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\qquad(\Delta\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times 2K}).`$ |  | (A.12) |

###### Proof.

Write $`\mathcal{L}=\mathcal{L}_{1}+\mathcal{L}_{2}+\mathrm{const}`$
with

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{L}_{1}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}):=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\bm{\Gamma}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\top}),\qquad\mathcal{L}_{2}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}):=-\,\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\bm{\Gamma}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{y\bar{z}}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\top}).
``` |  |

Using the Frobenius inner product
$`\langle A,B\rangle=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(A^{\top}B)`$ and
$`d(\bar{\mathbf{W}}\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\top})=(d\bar{\mathbf{W}})\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\top}+\bar{\mathbf{W}}\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\,(d\bar{\mathbf{W}})^{\top}`$
with $`\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}^{\top}=\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}`$,
$`\bm{\Gamma}^{\top}=\bm{\Gamma}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
d\mathcal{L}_{1}=\big\langle\bm{\Gamma}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}},\ d\bar{\mathbf{W}}\big\rangle,\qquad d\mathcal{L}_{2}=\big\langle-\,\bm{\Gamma}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{y\bar{z}},\ d\bar{\mathbf{W}}\big\rangle,
``` |  |

so
$`d\mathcal{L}=\langle\bm{\Gamma}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}_{y\bar{z}}),\,d\bar{\mathbf{W}}\rangle`$,
which proves
equation [A.11](#A1.E11 "Equation A.11 ‣ Lemma A.9 (Fisher two-channel quadratic: gradient and Hessian). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement").
For the Hessian, with
$`\bar{\mathbf{W}}(\epsilon)=\bar{\mathbf{W}}+\epsilon\Delta`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{d}{d\epsilon}\,\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(\epsilon))=\bm{\Gamma}\,\Delta\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}},
``` |  |

establishing
equation [A.12](#A1.E12 "Equation A.12 ‣ Lemma A.9 (Fisher two-channel quadratic: gradient and Hessian). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement").
Equivalently,
$`d^{2}\mathcal{L}[\Delta,\Delta]=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\Delta^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}\,\Delta\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}})=\|\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\Delta\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\|_{F}^{2}\geq 0`$.
∎

###### Lemma A.10 (PL inequality on $`{\bm{1}}^{\perp}`$).

Under mixture exploration
(Lemma [A.1](#A1.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma A.1 (Mixture curvature and Fisher bounds on 𝟏^⟂). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"))
so that
Lemma [A.7](#A1.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma A.7 (Population second moment is positive definite on 𝑆). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
holds, the population Fisher–weighted quadratic

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})\;=\;\tfrac{1}{2}\,\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\!\big(\bm{\Gamma}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\top}\big)\;-\;\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\!\big(\bm{\Gamma}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{y\bar{z}}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\top}\big)\;+\;\mathrm{const}
``` |  |

satisfies, for any global minimizer $`\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\star}`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})-\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\star})\ \leq\ \frac{1}{2\mu}\,\big\|\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})\big\|_{F}^{2},\qquad\mu\ :=\ \lambda_{\min}^{+}\!\big(\bm{\Gamma}\big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}\big)\ \lambda_{\min}^{+}\!\big(\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big|_{S}\big),
``` |  | (A.13) |

where $`S:={\bm{1}}^{\perp}\oplus{\bm{1}}^{\perp}`$. Moreover, by
Lemma [A.1](#A1.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma A.1 (Mixture curvature and Fisher bounds on 𝟏^⟂). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
and
Lemma [A.7](#A1.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma A.7 (Population second moment is positive definite on 𝑆). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lambda_{\min}^{+}\!\big(\bm{\Gamma}\big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}\big)\ \geq\ \frac{\gamma}{K},\qquad\lambda_{\min}^{+}\!\big(\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big|_{S}\big)\ \geq\ \underline{\sigma}\ >0,\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\mu\ \geq\ \frac{\gamma}{K}\cdot\underline{\sigma}.
``` |  | (A.14) |

###### Proof.

Let $`\Delta:=\bar{\mathbf{W}}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\star}`$ and define
$`\mathbf{X}:=\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\,\Delta\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}`$. By
Lemma [A.9](#A1.Thmtheorem9 "Lemma A.9 (Fisher two-channel quadratic: gradient and Hessian). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})\;=\;\bm{\Gamma}\,\Delta\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\;=\;\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\,\mathbf{X}\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2},\qquad\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})-\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\star})\;=\;\tfrac{1}{2}\,\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\!\big(\bm{\Gamma}\,\Delta\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\,\Delta^{\top}\big)\;=\;\tfrac{1}{2}\,\|\mathbf{X}\|_{F}^{2}.
``` |  |

For any PSD $`\mathbf{A},\mathbf{B}`$ and any matrix $`\mathbf{X}`$,
$`\|\,\mathbf{A}^{1/2}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{B}^{1/2}\,\|_{F}^{2}=\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\mathbf{X}^{\top}\mathbf{A}\mathbf{X}\mathbf{B})\geq\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{A})\lambda_{\min}(\mathbf{B})\|\mathbf{X}\|_{F}^{2}`$.
Applying this with $`\mathbf{A}=\bm{\Gamma}\big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}`$
and $`\mathbf{B}=\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big|_{S}`$, we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\big\|\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})\big\|_{F}^{2}=\big\|\bm{\Gamma}^{1/2}\mathbf{X}\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}^{1/2}\big\|_{F}^{2}\ \geq\ \lambda_{\min}^{+}\!\big(\bm{\Gamma}\big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}\big)\ \lambda_{\min}^{+}\!\big(\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big|_{S}\big)\ \|\mathbf{X}\|_{F}^{2}=2\,\mu\,\big(\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})-\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\star})\big),
``` |  |

which is equivalent to the PL inequality
equation [A.13](#A1.E13 "Equation A.13 ‣ Lemma A.10 (PL inequality on 𝟏^⟂). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement").
The bound
equation [A.14](#A1.E14 "Equation A.14 ‣ Lemma A.10 (PL inequality on 𝟏^⟂). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
follows from
Lemma [A.1](#A1.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma A.1 (Mixture curvature and Fisher bounds on 𝟏^⟂). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
(giving $`\bm{\Gamma}\big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}\succeq(\gamma/K)I`$) and
Lemma [A.7](#A1.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma A.7 (Population second moment is positive definite on 𝑆). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
(giving $`\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big|_{S}\succeq\underline{\sigma}I`$). ∎

### A.2 Reward-shock Robustness

###### Proof of Theorem [4.8](#S4.Thmtheorem8 "Theorem 4.8 (Stability to One-step Reward Perturbations). ‣ 4.1 Theoretical Guarantees for ICPO ‣ 4 Theoretical Framework for ICPO ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement").

Under the $`s`$-CRN coupling
(Def. [4.7](#S4.Thmtheorem7 "Definition 4.7 (𝑠-CRN coupled trajectories). ‣ 4.1 Theoretical Guarantees for ICPO ‣ 4 Theoretical Framework for ICPO ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")),
the baseline and perturbed runs share the same task $`\mathbf{w}`$, the
same uniforms $`\{U_{t}\}`$ for sampling, and the same noise sequence
$`\{\epsilon_{t}\}`$; they differ only by feeding a shocked reward
$`\widetilde{r}_{s}=r_{s}+\delta_{r}`$ at round $`s`$. Define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta\mathbf{p}_{t+1}:=\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}-\mathbf{p}_{t+1},\quad\Delta\mathbf{n}_{t}:=\widetilde{\mathbf{n}}_{t}-\mathbf{n}_{t},\quad\Delta\mathbf{g}_{t}:=\widetilde{\mathbf{g}}_{t}-\mathbf{g}_{t},\quad a_{t+1}:=\mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\mathbf{p}_{t+1}\|\,\mid\,\mathcal{F}_{s-1}],
``` |  |

and the normalized accumulators
$`\bar{\mathbf{n}}_{t}:=\mathbf{n}_{t}/t`$,
$`\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{t}:=\mathbf{g}_{t}/t`$ (and their deltas
analogously).

Pathwise relations for counts and rewards. With
$`\mathbf{x}_{t}=\mathbf{e}_{A_{t}}`$ and
$`r_{t}=\langle\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x}_{t}\rangle+\epsilon_{t}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbf{n}_{t}=\sum_{u=1}^{t}\mathbf{x}_{u},\qquad\mathbf{g}_{t}=\sum_{u=1}^{t}r_{u}\,\mathbf{x}_{u}=\Diag(\mathbf{n}_{t})\,\mathbf{w}+\sum_{u=1}^{t}\epsilon_{u}\,\mathbf{x}_{u}.
``` |  |

Let $`h_{t}:=\sum_{u=1}^{t}\epsilon_{u}\,\mathbf{x}_{u}`$ and
$`\widetilde{h}_{t}:=\sum_{u=1}^{t}\epsilon_{u}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{u}`$.
For the perturbed run, only round $`s`$ changes in the fed reward, hence

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widetilde{\mathbf{g}}_{t}=\Diag(\widetilde{\mathbf{n}}_{t})\,\mathbf{w}+\widetilde{h}_{t}+\mathbf{1}\{t\geq s\}\,\delta_{r}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{s}.
``` |  |

Therefore, for $`t\geq s`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{t}=\frac{1}{t}\,\Diag(\mathbf{w})\,\Delta\mathbf{n}_{t}+\frac{1}{t}\,\Delta h_{t}+\frac{1}{t}\,\delta_{r}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{s},\qquad\Delta h_{t}:=\widetilde{h}_{t}-h_{t}.
``` |  | (A.15) |

Two-channel linearization of projected logits. Using the
(population-optimal) one-step normal form aligned with
equation [3.2](#S3.E2 "Equation 3.2 ‣ Policy Optimization Framework. ‣ 3 Preliminaries ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
and projecting away the softmax gauge,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t+1}:=\text{Proj}\!\Big(\widehat{\mathbf{s}}^{(\mathrm{pert})}_{t+1}-\widehat{\mathbf{s}}^{(\mathrm{base})}_{t+1}\Big)=\frac{c}{t}\,\Big(\mathbf{V}\,\Delta\mathbf{n}_{t}+\mathbf{U}\,\Delta\mathbf{g}_{t}\Big)=c\Big(\mathbf{V}\,\Delta\bar{\mathbf{n}}_{t}+\mathbf{U}\,\Delta\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{t}\Big).
``` |  | (A.16) |

Softmax is $`1/2`$-Lipschitz on $`{\bm{1}}^{\perp}`$, and the
$`\gamma`$-mixture
equation [3.3](#S3.E3 "Equation 3.3 ‣ Policy Optimization Framework. ‣ 3 Preliminaries ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
scales the sensitivity by $`(1-\gamma)`$, hence

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\Delta\mathbf{p}_{t+1}\|\ \leq\ (1-\gamma)\,\frac{1}{2}\,\|\Delta\mathbf{y}_{t+1}\|.
``` |  | (A.17) |

Combining
equation [A.15](#A1.E15 "Equation A.15 ‣ Proof of Theorem . ‣ A.2 Reward-shock Robustness ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")–equation [A.17](#A1.E17 "Equation A.17 ‣ Proof of Theorem . ‣ A.2 Reward-shock Robustness ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
and conditioning on $`\mathcal{F}_{s-1}`$ yields

|  |  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|----|
|  | $`\displaystyle a_{t+1}\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{c(1-\gamma)}{2}\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\,\big\|\big(\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{U}\Diag(\mathbf{w})\big)\,\Delta\bar{\mathbf{n}}_{t}+\mathbf{U}\,\Delta\bar{h}_{t}+\mathbf{U}\,\frac{\delta_{r}}{t}\,\widetilde{\mathbf{x}}_{s}\big\|\ \Bigm|\ \mathcal{F}_{s-1}\right]`$ |  |  |
|  | $`\displaystyle\leq`$ | $`\displaystyle\frac{c(1-\gamma)}{2t}\,\Big(\|\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{U}\Diag(\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\ \mathbb{E}[\|\Delta\mathbf{n}_{t}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}]`$ |  |  |
|  |  | $`\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\ \mathbb{E}[\|\Delta h_{t}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}]+\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\,|\delta_{r}|\Big).`$ |  | (A.18) |

##### CRN coupling controls $`\mathbb{E}\|\Delta\mathbf{n}_{t}\|`$ and $`\mathbb{E}\|\Delta h_{t}\|`$.

By inverse-CDF coupling,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{P}\!\big(\widetilde{A}_{u}\neq A_{u}\mid\mathcal{F}_{u-1}\big)=\tfrac{1}{2}\|\Delta\mathbf{p}_{u}\|_{1}\ \leq\ \tfrac{\sqrt{K}}{2}\,\|\Delta\mathbf{p}_{u}\|.
``` |  |

Since $`\|\Delta\mathbf{x}_{u}\|\leq\sqrt{2}`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\!\big[\|\Delta\mathbf{x}_{u}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{u-1}\big]\ \leq\ \sqrt{\tfrac{K}{2}}\ \|\Delta\mathbf{p}_{u}\|.
``` |  | (A.19) |

Summing
equation [A.19](#A1.E19 "Equation A.19 ‣ CRN coupling controls 𝔼⁢‖Δ⁢𝐧_𝑡‖ and 𝔼⁢‖Δ⁢ℎ_𝑡‖. ‣ A.2 Reward-shock Robustness ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
from $`u=s`$ to $`t`$ and conditioning on $`\mathcal{F}_{s-1}`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\!\big[\|\Delta\mathbf{n}_{t}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}\big]\ \leq\ \sqrt{\tfrac{K}{2}}\sum_{u=s}^{t}a_{u}.
``` |  | (A.20) |

For the noise accumulator, using the zero-mean
$`\sigma_{\epsilon}`$-sub-Gaussian assumption (hence
$`\mathbb{E}[|\epsilon_{u}|\,|\,\mathcal{F}_{u-1}]\leq C_{\epsilon}:=\sqrt{2/\pi}\,\sigma_{\epsilon}`$)
gives

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\!\big[\|\Delta h_{t}\|\mid\mathcal{F}_{s-1}\big]\ \leq\ C_{\epsilon}\,\sqrt{\tfrac{K}{2}}\sum_{u=s}^{t}a_{u}.
``` |  | (A.21) |

##### A Grönwall-type recursion and its solution.

Plugging
equation [A.20](#A1.E20 "Equation A.20 ‣ CRN coupling controls 𝔼⁢‖Δ⁢𝐧_𝑡‖ and 𝔼⁢‖Δ⁢ℎ_𝑡‖. ‣ A.2 Reward-shock Robustness ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")–equation [A.21](#A1.E21 "Equation A.21 ‣ CRN coupling controls 𝔼⁢‖Δ⁢𝐧_𝑡‖ and 𝔼⁢‖Δ⁢ℎ_𝑡‖. ‣ A.2 Reward-shock Robustness ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
into
equation [A.18](#A1.E18 "Equation A.18 ‣ Proof of Theorem . ‣ A.2 Reward-shock Robustness ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
define

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a\ :=\ \frac{c(1-\gamma)}{2}\,\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\mathrm{op}},\qquad b\ :=\ \frac{c(1-\gamma)}{2}\,\sqrt{\frac{K}{2}}\,\Big(\ \|\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{U}\Diag(\mathbf{w})\|_{\mathrm{op}}\ +\ C_{\epsilon}\,\|\mathbf{U}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\ \Big),
``` |  |

and obtain, for $`t\geq s`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{t+1}\ \leq\ \frac{a}{t}\,|\delta_{r}|\ +\ \frac{b}{t}\,\sum_{u=s}^{t}a_{u}.
``` |  | (A.22) |

Let $`S_{t}:=\sum_{u=s}^{t}a_{u}`$ with $`S_{s-1}=0`$. Then

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
S_{t+1}\ \leq\ \Big(1+\frac{b}{t}\Big)\,S_{t}\ +\ \frac{a}{t}\,|\delta_{r}|.
``` |  | (A.23) |

Unrolling
equation [A.23](#A1.E23 "Equation A.23 ‣ A Grönwall-type recursion and its solution. ‣ A.2 Reward-shock Robustness ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
and using the standard Gamma-ratio bound yields a constant $`C_{b}>0`$
(depending only on $`b`$) such that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
S_{t}\ \leq\ a\,C_{b}\,|\delta_{r}|\sum_{u=s}^{t-1}\frac{1}{u}\left(\frac{t}{u}\right)^{\!b}\ \leq\ \frac{a\,C_{b}}{b}\,\Big(\frac{t}{s}\Big)^{\!b}\,|\delta_{r}|.
``` |  |

Returning to
equation [A.22](#A1.E22 "Equation A.22 ‣ A Grönwall-type recursion and its solution. ‣ A.2 Reward-shock Robustness ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
a_{t+1}\ \leq\ \frac{a}{t}\,|\delta_{r}|\ +\ \frac{b}{t}\,S_{t}\ \leq\ \frac{a}{t}\,|\delta_{r}|\ +\ \frac{a\,C_{b}}{t}\,\Big(\frac{t}{s}\Big)^{\!b}\,|\delta_{r}|\ =\ \frac{a(1+C_{b})}{s}\,\Big(\frac{t}{s}\Big)^{\!b-1}\,|\delta_{r}|.
``` |  |

Therefore, for any $`1\leq s\leq t`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\ \|\Delta\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}^{s}\|_{2}\ \bigm|\ \mathcal{F}_{s-1}\right]\ \leq\ \frac{a(1+C_{b})}{s}\left(\frac{t}{s}\right)^{b-1}|\delta_{r}|.
``` |  | (A.24) |

In particular, if the learning-rate constant $`c`$ is small enough so
that $`b<1`$, then $`\left(\tfrac{t}{s}\right)^{b-1}\!\to 0`$ as
$`t\to\infty`$, and the one-shot impact decays to zero:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lim_{t\to\infty}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\ \|\Delta\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}^{s}\|_{2}\ \bigm|\ \mathcal{F}_{s-1}\right]=0.
``` |  |

This completes the proof. ∎

### A.3 KL Divergence vs. Fisher-weighted Duadratic

###### Proof of Theorem [4.1](#S4.Thmtheorem1 "Theorem 4.1 (mixed-policy KL is controlled by the Fisher-projected quadratic loss). ‣ Training Objective. ‣ 4 Theoretical Framework for ICPO ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement").

Recall that $`N`$ denotes the trajectory length in the dataset
construction of
Sec. [4](#S4 "4 Theoretical Framework for ICPO ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
and expectations below average over $`(\tau,t)`$ with
$`t\in\{1,\dots,N-1\}`$ and $`\tau\in\mathcal{D}`$. Let
$`\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{s}):=\mathrm{softmax}(\mathbf{s})`$ and
define the projected logit error

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\Delta_{t+1}\ :=\ \text{Proj}\big(\widehat{\mathbf{s}}_{t+1}-\mathbf{s}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}\big)\in{\bm{1}}^{\perp},\qquad\text{Proj}:=\mathbf{I}-\tfrac{1}{K}{\bm{1}}{\bm{1}}^{\top}.
``` |  |

Write the (softmax) Fisher matrix as
$`\mathbf{F}(\mathbf{p}):=\Diag(\mathbf{p})-\mathbf{p}\mathbf{p}^{\top}`$
and recall from
Eq. equation [4.1](#S4.E1 "Equation 4.1 ‣ Training Objective. ‣ 4 Theoretical Framework for ICPO ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
that

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{L}(\bm{\theta})\;=\;\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{1}{N-1}\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}\,\Delta_{t+1}\right],\qquad\bm{\Gamma}\ :=\ \frac{1}{N-1}\mathbb{E}\!\left[\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}\mathbf{F}\!\big(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t}\big)\right].
``` |  |

By
Lemma [A.1](#A1.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma A.1 (Mixture curvature and Fisher bounds on 𝟏^⟂). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
(mixture curvature), along the teacher’s $`\gamma`$-mixture policy we
have the spectral sandwich on $`{\bm{1}}^{\perp}`$:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{\gamma}{K}\,\mathbf{I}\ \preceq\ \mathbf{F}\!\big(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t}\big)\Big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}\ \preceq\ \frac{1}{2}\,\mathbf{I}.
``` |  | (A.25) |

By convexity of the PSD cone, the same bounds transfer to
$`\bm{\Gamma}`$ on $`{\bm{1}}^{\perp}`$.

##### Upper bound.

Mixing with the uniform distribution is a Markov kernel; by data
processing for $`f`$-divergences,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\big(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}\,\|\,\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}\big)\ \leq\ D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{s}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1})\,\|\,\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}(\widehat{\mathbf{s}}_{t+1})\big)=D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{s}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1})\,\|\,\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{s}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}+\Delta_{t+1})\big).
``` |  |

Let $`\phi(\mathbf{s}):=\log\sum_{i}e^{s_{i}}`$; then
$`\nabla\phi=\mathrm{softmax}`$ and
$`\nabla^{2}\phi(\mathbf{s})=\mathbf{F}(\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{s}))`$.
The Bregman integral form of KL yields, for any
$`\Delta\in{\bm{1}}^{\perp}`$,

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{s})\,\|\,\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{s}+\Delta)\big)=\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau)\,\Delta^{\top}\mathbf{F}\!\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{s}+\tau\Delta)\big)\,\Delta\,d\tau.
``` |  | (A.26) |

Using
$`\|\mathbf{F}(\cdot)\big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}`$
and $`\int_{0}^{1}(1-\tau)\,d\tau=\tfrac{1}{2}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\big(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}\,\|\,\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}\big)\ \leq\ \tfrac{1}{4}\,\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}.
``` |  |

From
equation [A.25](#A1.E25 "Equation A.25 ‣ Proof of Theorem . ‣ A.3 KL Divergence vs. Fisher-weighted Duadratic ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
$`\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}\,\Delta_{t+1}\ \geq\ \tfrac{\gamma}{K}\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}`$,
hence
$`\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}\ \leq\ \tfrac{K}{\gamma}\,\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}\,\Delta_{t+1}`$.
Averaging over $`t`$ and taking expectation,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{1}{N-1}\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\big(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}\,\|\,\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}\big)\right]\ \leq\ \frac{K}{4\gamma}\,\mathcal{L}(\bm{\theta}).
``` |  |

##### Lower bound.

Pinsker’s inequality implies
$`D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathbf{p}\|\mathbf{q})\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}\|_{1}^{2}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\|\mathbf{p}-\mathbf{q}\|_{2}^{2}`$.
Let
$`f(\mathbf{s}):=(1-\gamma)\,\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{s})+\gamma\,{\bm{1}}/K`$
so that
$`\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}=f(\mathbf{s}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1})`$ and
$`\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}=f(\widehat{\mathbf{s}}_{t+1})`$. By the
mean-value integral,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}-\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}=\int_{0}^{1}\nabla f(\mathbf{s}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}+\tau\Delta_{t+1})\,\Delta_{t+1}\,d\tau=(1-\gamma)\!\int_{0}^{1}\mathbf{F}\!\big(\widetilde{\mathbf{p}}(\mathbf{s}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}+\tau\Delta_{t+1})\big)\,\Delta_{t+1}\,d\tau,
``` |  |

whence
$`\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}-\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}\|_{2}\leq(1-\gamma)\cdot\tfrac{1}{2}\,\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{2}`$
and thus

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}\ \geq\ \frac{4}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\,\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}-\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}.
``` |  |

Using the upper side of
equation [A.25](#A1.E25 "Equation A.25 ‣ Proof of Theorem . ‣ A.3 KL Divergence vs. Fisher-weighted Duadratic ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
$`\Delta_{t+1}^{\top}\bm{\Gamma}\,\Delta_{t+1}\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\,\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}`$,
we obtain

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{L}(\bm{\theta})\ \leq\ \frac{1}{2}\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{1}{N-1}\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}\|\Delta_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}\right]\ \leq\ \frac{2}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{1}{N-1}\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}-\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}\right].
``` |  |

Finally, combining with Pinsker yields

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{1}{N-1}\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}D_{\mathrm{KL}}\!\big(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}\,\|\,\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}\big)\right]\geq\frac{1}{2}\,\mathbb{E}\!\left[\frac{1}{N-1}\sum_{t=1}^{N-1}\|\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}-\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}\right]\geq\frac{(1-\gamma)^{2}}{4}\,\mathcal{L}(\bm{\theta}).
``` |  |

This proves the two-sided bound. ∎

### A.4 Closed-Loop Imitation of Policy Optimization

###### Proof of Theorem [4.2](#S4.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 4.2 (Population Equivalence). ‣ 4.1 Theoretical Guarantees for ICPO ‣ 4 Theoretical Framework for ICPO ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement").

By
Lemma [A.4](#A1.Thmtheorem4 "Lemma A.4 (Two-channel projected logits: exact equality). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
the *projected* student logits are linear in the normalized statistics:

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\text{Proj}\,\widehat{\mathbf{s}}_{t+1}\;=\;\bar{\mathbf{W}}\,\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t},\qquad\bar{\mathbf{W}}:=[\,\mathbf{W}_{n}\ \ \mathbf{W}_{g}\,]\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times 2K},\quad\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}:=\binom{\bar{\mathbf{n}}_{t}}{\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{t}}\in\mathbb{R}^{2K}.
``` |  |

For the teacher generated by
equation [3.2](#S3.E2 "Equation 3.2 ‣ Policy Optimization Framework. ‣ 3 Preliminaries ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
with $`\eta_{t}=c/t`$, we likewise have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbf{y}_{t+1}:=\text{Proj}\,\mathbf{s}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}\;=\;\frac{c}{t}\,\text{Proj}\!\big(\mathbf{V}\,\mathbf{n}_{t}+\mathbf{U}\,\mathbf{g}_{t}\big)\;=\;\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star}\,\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t},\qquad\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star}:=\text{Proj}\,[\,\mathbf{V}\ \ \mathbf{U}\,]\in\mathbb{R}^{K\times 2K},
``` |  |

so the *labels are realizable* by the same two-channel structure.

Lemma [A.5](#A1.Thmtheorem5 "Lemma A.5 (Fisher–weighted quadratic in the two-channel operator). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
gives the population Fisher–weighted quadratic form

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{L}(\bm{\theta})=\frac{1}{2}\,\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\bm{\Gamma}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\top})-\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}(\bm{\Gamma}\,\bm{\Sigma}_{y\bar{z}}\,\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\top})+\text{const},\qquad\bm{\Gamma}:=\mathbb{E}\!\big[\mathbf{F}(\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1})\big],\ \ \bar{\bm{\Sigma}}:=\mathbb{E}[\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}^{\top}].
``` |  |

Using realizability
$`\mathbf{y}_{t+1}=\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star}\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}`$, we
have
$`\bm{\Sigma}_{y\bar{z}}=\mathbb{E}[\mathbf{y}_{t+1}\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}^{\top}]=\mathbb{E}[\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star}\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}^{\top}]=\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star}\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}`$.
Substituting gives the *completed-square* form

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{L}(\bm{\theta})=\frac{1}{2}\,\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\!\Big(\bm{\Gamma}\,(\bar{\mathbf{W}}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star})\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\,(\bar{\mathbf{W}}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star})^{\top}\Big)+\text{const}.
``` |  |

By
Lemma [A.1](#A1.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma A.1 (Mixture curvature and Fisher bounds on 𝟏^⟂). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
(mixture curvature),
$`\bm{\Gamma}\big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}\succeq(\gamma/K)\,I`$; by
Lemma [A.7](#A1.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma A.7 (Population second moment is positive definite on 𝑆). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
$`\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big|_{S}\succeq\underline{\sigma}\,I`$ on
$`S:={\bm{1}}^{\perp}\oplus{\bm{1}}^{\perp}`$ under
Assumption [3.1](#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Assumption 3.1 (Data Coverage and Signal Dominance). ‣ Supervised Pretraining Data Generation. ‣ 3 Preliminaries ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement").
Hence the quadratic is *strongly convex* in $`\bar{\mathbf{W}}`$ on
$`S`$ and has the unique minimizer
$`\bar{\mathbf{W}}=\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star}`$. Therefore, for any
history prefix $`\mathcal{H}_{t}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\text{Proj}\,\widehat{\mathbf{s}}_{t+1}(\mathcal{H}_{t};\bm{\theta}^{\star})=\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star}\,\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}=\text{Proj}\,\mathbf{s}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}(\mathcal{H}_{t}).
``` |  |

Since softmax is invariant to additive constants (the projected logits
remove exactly the $`\mathrm{span}\{{\bm{1}}\}`$ gauge), the induced
policies coincide:
$`\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}(\mathcal{H}_{t};\bm{\theta}^{\star})=\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}(\mathcal{H}_{t})`$.
∎

###### Proof of Theorem [4.3](#S4.Thmtheorem3 "Theorem 4.3 (Finite sample result). ‣ 4.1 Theoretical Guarantees for ICPO ‣ 4 Theoretical Framework for ICPO ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement").

By
Lemma [A.4](#A1.Thmtheorem4 "Lemma A.4 (Two-channel projected logits: exact equality). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
the projected student logits are linear in the normalized statistics:
$`\text{Proj}\,\widehat{\mathbf{s}}_{t+1}=\bar{\mathbf{W}}\,\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}`$
with $`\bar{\mathbf{W}}=[\mathbf{W}_{n}\ \mathbf{W}_{g}]`$ and
$`\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}=(\bar{\mathbf{n}}_{t}^{\top},\bar{\mathbf{g}}_{t}^{\top})^{\top}`$.
Let $`\mathbf{y}_{t+1}:=\text{Proj}\,(\mathbf{s}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1})`$.
Lemma [A.5](#A1.Thmtheorem5 "Lemma A.5 (Fisher–weighted quadratic in the two-channel operator). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
and
Lemma [A.6](#A1.Thmtheorem6 "Lemma A.6 (Empirical Fisher–weighted quadratic form). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
give the population and empirical Fisher–weighted quadratic forms, and
Theorem [4.2](#S4.Thmtheorem2 "Theorem 4.2 (Population Equivalence). ‣ 4.1 Theoretical Guarantees for ICPO ‣ 4 Theoretical Framework for ICPO ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
(under
Assumption [3.1](#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Assumption 3.1 (Data Coverage and Signal Dominance). ‣ Supervised Pretraining Data Generation. ‣ 3 Preliminaries ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"))
guarantees realizability: there exists a unique
$`\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star}`$ such that
$`\mathbf{y}_{t+1}=\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star}\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}`$ for
all prefixes. Moreover,
Lemma [A.1](#A1.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma A.1 (Mixture curvature and Fisher bounds on 𝟏^⟂). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
implies $`\bm{\Gamma}\big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}\succeq(\gamma/K)I`$, and
Lemma [A.7](#A1.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma A.7 (Population second moment is positive definite on 𝑆). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
implies $`\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big|_{S}\succeq\underline{\sigma}I`$ with
$`\underline{\sigma}\asymp c_{\lambda}/t`$ on
$`S:={\bm{1}}^{\perp}\oplus{\bm{1}}^{\perp}`$, so the population
quadratic in $`\bar{\mathbf{W}}`$ is strongly convex on $`S`$ with
unique minimizer $`\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star}`$. For the empirical
problem,
Lemma [A.8](#A1.Thmtheorem8 "Lemma A.8 (Sample second-moment concentration for 𝐳̄_𝑡). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
yields (for i.i.d. rollouts)

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\|\widehat{\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}}-\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\|_{\mathrm{op}}\ \leq\ C_{1}L^{2}\Big(\sqrt{\tfrac{2K+\log(1/\delta)}{M}}+\tfrac{2K+\log(1/\delta)}{M}\Big).
``` |  |

Taking $`M\geq C\,t^{2}(2K+\log(1/\delta))/c_{\lambda}^{2}`$ (for a
large enough absolute $`C`$ depending on $`C_{1},L`$) guarantees
$`\widehat{\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}}\big|_{S}\succeq\tfrac{1}{2}\,\underline{\sigma}I`$,
while
$`\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}\big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}\succeq(\gamma/K)I`$ by
mixture curvature. Plugging the realizable labels
$`\mathbf{y}_{t+1}=\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star}\bar{\mathbf{z}}_{t}`$ into
Lemma [A.6](#A1.Thmtheorem6 "Lemma A.6 (Empirical Fisher–weighted quadratic form). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
gives

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\widehat{\mathcal{L}}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})=\tfrac{1}{2}\,\mathop{\mathrm{tr}}\!\big(\widehat{\bm{\Gamma}}\,(\bar{\mathbf{W}}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star})\,\widehat{\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}}\,(\bar{\mathbf{W}}-\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star})^{\top}\big)+\text{const},
``` |  |

so the empirical minimizer satisfies
$`\bar{\mathbf{W}}=\bar{\mathbf{W}}_{\star}`$ whenever
$`\widehat{\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}}\big|_{S}\succ 0`$ (which holds with
probability $`\geq 1-\delta`$). Thus
$`\text{Proj}\,\widehat{\mathbf{s}}_{t+1}=\text{Proj}\,\mathbf{s}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}`$
and consequently
$`\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}(\mathcal{H}_{t};\widehat{\bm{\theta}})=\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}(\mathcal{H}_{t})`$
for the fixed test history $`\mathcal{H}_{t}`$. For the expected
mismatch, on the high-probability event the error is $`0`$, and on its
complement a crude bound together with the $`(1-\gamma)`$ mixture factor
yields
$`\|\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}-\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}\leq 2(1-\gamma)^{2}`$,
hence

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathbb{E}_{\mathrm{train}}\big[\|\widehat{\mathbf{p}}_{t+1}-\mathbf{p}^{\mathrm{PO}}_{t+1}\|_{2}^{2}\big]\ \leq\ 0\cdot(1-\delta)+2(1-\gamma)^{2}\cdot\delta=2(1-\gamma)^{2}\delta.
``` |  |

This proves the theorem. ∎

### A.5 Convergence of the Fisher-trained Two-channel LSA

We analyze the continuous-time gradient flow on the two-channel operator
$`\bar{\mathbf{W}}`$ for the Fisher-weighted quadratic objective in
equation [A.10](#A1.E10 "Equation A.10 ‣ Lemma A.9 (Fisher two-channel quadratic: gradient and Hessian). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement").

###### Theorem A.11 (Exponential convergence of the $`\bar{\mathbf{W}}`$–flow).

Assume mixture exploration and that
Assumption [3.1](#S3.Thmtheorem1 "Assumption 3.1 (Data Coverage and Signal Dominance). ‣ Supervised Pretraining Data Generation. ‣ 3 Preliminaries ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
Lemma [A.1](#A1.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma A.1 (Mixture curvature and Fisher bounds on 𝟏^⟂). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
and
Lemma [A.7](#A1.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma A.7 (Population second moment is positive definite on 𝑆). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
hold. Consider the gradient flow for the *population* Fisher–weighted
quadratic $`\mathcal{L}`$:

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\dot{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}(t)\ =\ -\,\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\mathcal{L}\big(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t)\big)\ =\ -\,\bm{\Gamma}\big(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t)\,\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}_{y\bar{z}}\big),
``` |  | (A.27) |

initialized at any $`\bar{\mathbf{W}}(0)`$. Let
$`\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\star}`$ be a global minimizer of $`\mathcal{L}`$.
Then $`\mathcal{L}\big(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t)\big)`$ is strictly decreasing
along the flow and

|  |  |  |  |
|----|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{L}\big(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t)\big)-\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\star})\ \leq\ \exp(-2\mu\,t)\,\big(\mathcal{L}\big(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(0)\big)-\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\star})\big),
``` |  | (A.28) |

with

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mu\ :=\ \lambda_{\min}^{+}\!\big(\bm{\Gamma}\big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}\big)\;\lambda_{\min}^{+}\!\big(\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big|_{S}\big),\qquad S:={\bm{1}}^{\perp}\oplus{\bm{1}}^{\perp},
``` |  |

and, by
Lemma [A.1](#A1.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma A.1 (Mixture curvature and Fisher bounds on 𝟏^⟂). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
and
Lemma [A.7](#A1.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma A.7 (Population second moment is positive definite on 𝑆). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lambda_{\min}^{+}\!\big(\bm{\Gamma}\big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}\big)\ \geq\ \frac{\gamma}{K},\qquad\lambda_{\min}^{+}\!\big(\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big|_{S}\big)\ \geq\ \underline{\sigma}\ >\ 0,\qquad\Rightarrow\qquad\mu\ \geq\ \frac{\gamma}{K}\cdot\underline{\sigma}.
``` |  |

###### Proof of Theorem [A.11](#A1.Thmtheorem11 "Theorem A.11 (Exponential convergence of the 𝐖̄–flow). ‣ A.5 Convergence of the Fisher-trained Two-channel LSA ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement").

By
Lemma [A.9](#A1.Thmtheorem9 "Lemma A.9 (Fisher two-channel quadratic: gradient and Hessian). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
we have

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})=\bm{\Gamma}\big(\bar{\mathbf{W}}\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}-\bm{\Sigma}_{y\bar{z}}\big),
``` |  |

so the gradient flow
$`\dot{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}(t)=-\,\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t))`$
is well-defined. Along the trajectory,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{d}{dt}\,\mathcal{L}\big(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t)\big)=\big\langle\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\mathcal{L}\big(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t)\big),\ \dot{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}(t)\big\rangle=-\,\big\|\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\mathcal{L}\big(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t)\big)\big\|_{F}^{2}\ \leq\ 0,
``` |  |

hence $`\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t))`$ is nonincreasing and strictly
decreasing whenever
$`\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t))\neq{\bm{0}}`$.

Next, apply the Polyak–Łojasiewicz (PL) inequality on the restricted
subspace $`S:={\bm{1}}^{\perp}\oplus{\bm{1}}^{\perp}`$
(Lemma [A.10](#A1.Thmtheorem10 "Lemma A.10 (PL inequality on 𝟏^⟂). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")).
Let

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mu\ :=\ \lambda_{\min}^{+}\!\big(\bm{\Gamma}\big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}\big)\;\lambda_{\min}^{+}\!\big(\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big|_{S}\big).
``` |  |

Then for any global minimizer $`\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\star}`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})-\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\star})\ \leq\ \frac{1}{2\mu}\,\big\|\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}})\big\|_{F}^{2}.
``` |  |

Combining this with the energy decay identity gives, for all
$`t\geq 0`$,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\frac{d}{dt}\,\big(\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t))-\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\star})\big)\ =\ -\,\big\|\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{W}}}\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t))\big\|_{F}^{2}\ \leq\ -\,2\mu\,\big(\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t))-\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\star})\big).
``` |  |

By Grönwall’s inequality,

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\mathcal{L}\big(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(t)\big)-\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\star})\ \leq\ \exp(-2\mu\,t)\,\big(\mathcal{L}\big(\bar{\mathbf{W}}(0)\big)-\mathcal{L}(\bar{\mathbf{W}}^{\star})\big).
``` |  |

Finally, by mixture exploration
(Lemma [A.1](#A1.Thmtheorem1 "Lemma A.1 (Mixture curvature and Fisher bounds on 𝟏^⟂). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"))
and the lower bound on the population second moment on $`S`$
(Lemma [A.7](#A1.Thmtheorem7 "Lemma A.7 (Population second moment is positive definite on 𝑆). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")),

|  |  |  |
|----|----|----|
|  |
``` math
\lambda_{\min}^{+}\!\big(\bm{\Gamma}\big|_{{\bm{1}}^{\perp}}\big)\ \geq\ \frac{\gamma}{K},\qquad\lambda_{\min}^{+}\!\big(\bar{\bm{\Sigma}}\big|_{S}\big)\ \geq\ \underline{\sigma}\ >0,
``` |  |

which implies $`\mu\geq(\gamma/K)\,\underline{\sigma}`$. Strict decrease
of the loss holds except at stationary points; by
Lemma [A.9](#A1.Thmtheorem9 "Lemma A.9 (Fisher two-channel quadratic: gradient and Hessian). ‣ A.1 Preliminaries and Key Lemmas ‣ Appendix A Proofs and Technical Details for Section ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
together with the restricted positive definiteness, these coincide with
the global minimizers on $`{\bm{1}}^{\perp}`$. ∎

## Appendix B Experimental Details

This section provides additional details regarding the experimental
setup, including specific hyperparameters for our method and the
baselines, as well as the hardware and software environment used for all
experiments. We also present supplementary experimental results that
complement the main text.

### B.1 Hyperparameter Sensitivity.

Figure [4](#A2.F4 "Figure 4 ‣ B.1 Hyperparameter Sensitivity. ‣ Appendix B Experimental Details ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
shows the impact of varying the number of in-context optimization rounds
($`n`$) and candidates per round ($`k`$) on AIME 2024. As shown in
Figure [4(a)](#A2.F4.sf1 "Figure 4(a) ‣ Figure 4 ‣ B.1 Hyperparameter Sensitivity. ‣ Appendix B Experimental Details ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
performance improves substantially as the number of candidates ($`k`$)
increases from 2 to 64, with Maj@16 accuracy more than doubling, then
saturates beyond this point. Increasing the number of rounds ($`n`$) is
broadly beneficial up to a peak near $`n=5`$. A complementary grid and
the corresponding latency/VRAM measures are reported in
Appendix [C.2](#A3.SS2 "C.2 Empirical Cost ‣ Appendix C Detailed Complexity Analysis ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
(Tables [8](#A3.T8 "Table 8 ‣ results. ‣ C.2 Empirical Cost ‣ Appendix C Detailed Complexity Analysis ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")).

![Refer to caption](2603.01335v1/picture/qwen25_aime_sweep_k.png)

(a) Varying candidates $`k`$ (fixed $`n=5`$)

![Refer to caption](2603.01335v1/picture/qwen25_aime_sweep_n.png)

(b) Varying rounds $`n`$ (fixed $`k=16`$)

Figure 4: Hyperparameter sensitivity of ME-ICPO on AIME 2024 with
Qwen2.5-Math-7B.

### B.2 Hyperparameter and Implementation Details

##### ME-ICPO (Our Method).

For our method, we use the primary settings described in the main text
($`n=5`$ rounds of optimization, $`k=16`$ candidates per round).
Candidate chains-of-thought are sampled using a temperature of 0.6 and a
top-p value of 0.95. The summarization step, which is a crucial
component for managing context length, uses greedy decoding and is
prompted to produce a concise summary of approximately 100 tokens, with
a hard limit of 500 tokens, focusing on the reasoning strategy. The
predictive answer distribution, used for the entropy calculation, is
also estimated with a temperature of 0.6. The full text for all system
and summarization prompts is provided in
Appendix [D](#A4 "Appendix D Prompt Templates and Qualitative Examples ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement").

### B.3 Hardware and Environment Configuration

All experiments were conducted on a single server node equipped with 8
NVIDIA L40S GPUs, each with 48GB of HBM2e memory. Our implementation
uses PyTorch 2.2, vLLM 0.10.0, and Transformers 4.55.0. The operating
system is Red Hat Enterprise Linux 9.6, and the environment is managed
via Conda with Python 3.11.7 and CUDA 12.9.

### B.4 Supplementary Experimental Results

This section contains supplementary results and comparisons added during
the rebuttal phase to address specific computational and scalability
questions.

#### B.4.1 Computational Cost and Baseline Comparison

To assess efficiency, we compare ME-ICPO against standard prompt-style
test-time search baselines—Tree of Thoughts (ToT) ([Yao et al.,
2023a](#bib.bib5)) and Monte-Carlo Tree Refinement (MCTR) ([Zhang et
al., 2024a](#bib.bib25))—and the training-based test-time scaling method
TTRL ([Zuo et al., 2025](#bib.bib1)). For fairness, ME-ICPO uses a fixed
configuration of $`N{=}5`$ refinement rounds and $`k{=}16`$ samples per
round, averaged over 5 seeds. We report average inference time (seconds
per question) on AIME 2024. Because ToT/MCTR employ different search
depths and branching factors, their runs are not strictly time-matched;
for TTRL, we control the experiment to use a similar GPU hours as
ME-ICPO. As shown in
Table [3](#A2.T3 "Table 3 ‣ B.4.3 Harder Task Generalization ‣ B.4 Supplementary Experimental Results ‣ Appendix B Experimental Details ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
and
Table [4](#A2.T4 "Table 4 ‣ B.4.3 Harder Task Generalization ‣ B.4 Supplementary Experimental Results ‣ Appendix B Experimental Details ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement"),
ME-ICPO achieves top-tier accuracy and Mean@16 at a competitive compute
budget, reflecting principled gains from the underlying Policy
Optimization mechanism. Shallow-search prompt methods (ToT/MCTR) can be
faster when search depth is limited but lag notably in accuracy, while
under matched wall-clock budgets ME-ICPO attains higher Acc/Mean@16 than
TTRL, demonstrating more efficient scaling; importantly, ICPO provides a
mechanistic account of test-time self-refinement as policy optimization
rather than a heuristic leaderboard tweak.

#### B.4.2 Frontier Model Scalability

To demonstrate the versatility of our framework, we evaluate ME-ICPO on
recent frontier models, including Qwen3-4B-Instruct ([Yang et al.,
2024b](#bib.bib46)) (a long-CoT specialized model), and the Gemini-2.5
series (Pro and Flash) ([Comanici et al., 2025](#bib.bib47)).
Tables [5](#A2.T5 "Table 5 ‣ B.4.3 Harder Task Generalization ‣ B.4 Supplementary Experimental Results ‣ Appendix B Experimental Details ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
show that ME-ICPO consistently enhances performance across these diverse
architectures, confirming its scalability.

#### B.4.3 Harder Task Generalization

We further evaluate performance on exceptionally difficult,
high-school/collegiate level math competition tasks, specifically the
Harvard-MIT Mathematics Tournament (HMMT) ([Balunović et al.,
2025](#bib.bib48)) and the APEX-shortlist dataset ([Balunović et al.,
2025](#bib.bib48)). Results in
Table [6](#A2.T6 "Table 6 ‣ B.4.3 Harder Task Generalization ‣ B.4 Supplementary Experimental Results ‣ Appendix B Experimental Details ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
and
Table [7](#A2.T7 "Table 7 ‣ B.4.3 Harder Task Generalization ‣ B.4 Supplementary Experimental Results ‣ Appendix B Experimental Details ‣ Provable and Practical In-Context Policy Optimization for Self-Improvement")
indicate that ME-ICPO provides robustness even on tasks with low
baseline solve rates.
````
